**Working**

Let the missing term $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \div 3 - 1 &= 8 \\\\
a \div 3     &= 8 + 1 \\\\
\dfrac{a}{3} &= 9 \\\\
a            &= 9 \times 3 \\\\
a            &= 27
\end{aligned}
$
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

$a$ and $b$ are two *different* positive whole numbers which make the following statement true

$3a + 2b = 30$ 

Find two possible pairs of numbers which make the statement true.

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">$a$ and $b$ are two *different* positive whole numbers which make the following statement true
<emptyline>
$3a + 2b = 30$ 
<emptyline>
Find two possible pairs of numbers which make the statement true.
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

For $a =10$,

$
\begin{aligned}
3a + 2b                             &= 30 \\\\
3 \times 10 + 2b                    &= 30 \\\\
30 + 2b                             &= 30 \\\\
2b                                  &= 30 - 30 \\\\
2b                                  &= 0 \\\\
b                                   &= 0
\end{aligned}
$

For $a= 2$,

$
\begin{aligned}
3a + 2b                             &= 30 \\\\
3 \times 2 + 2b                     &= 30 \\\\
2b                                  &= 30 - 6 \\\\
2b                                  &= 24 \\\\
b                                   &= \dfrac {24} {2} \\\\
b                                   &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
b                                   &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$a = 10,b = 0 \\
a = 2,b = 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class="workingscodeblock">
<div class="tipcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="tipcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Tips Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="formulacodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="formulacodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Formulas Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workingcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="workingcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Working Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">For $a =10$,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3a + 2b                             &= 30 \\\\
3 \times 10 + 2b                    &= 30 \\\\
30 + 2b                             &= 30 \\\\
2b                                  &= 30 - 30 \\\\
2b                                  &= 0 \\\\
b                                   &= 0
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
For $a= 2$,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3a + 2b                             &= 30 \\\\
3 \times 2 + 2b                     &= 30 \\\\
2b                                  &= 30 - 6 \\\\
2b                                  &= 24 \\\\
b                                   &= \dfrac {24} {2} \\\\
b                                   &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
b                                   &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answercodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="answercodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Option Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="answercodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="answercodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Key Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="answercodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="answercodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Answer Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">$a = 10,b = 0 \\
a = 2,b = 12$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class="papername">

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

$p$ and $q$ are whole numbers. $p$ is $150$ greater than $q$.

The following statement is also true
                       
$\large p + q = 1000$

Calculate the numbers $p$ and $q$.

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">$p$ and $q$ are whole numbers. $p$ is $150$ greater than $q$.
<emptyline>
The following statement is also true
<emptyline>
$\large p + q = 1000$
<emptyline>
Calculate the numbers $p$ and $q$.
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

$p = 150 + q$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
p + q         &= 1000 \\\\
150 + q + q   &= 1000 \\\\
150 + 2 q     &= 1000 \\\\
2 q           &= 1000 - 150 \\\\
2 q           &= 850 \\\\
q             &= \dfrac {850} {2} \\\\
q             &= \dfrac {425 \times 2} {2} \\\\
q             &= \dfrac {425 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
q             &= 425
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
p  &= 150 + q \\\\
p  &= 150 + 425 \\\\
p  &= 575
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$p = 575, q= 425$

</div>
</div>
<div class="workingscodeblock">
<div class="tipcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="tipcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Tips Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="formulacodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="formulacodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Formulas Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workingcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="workingcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Working Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">$p = 150 + q$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
p + q         &= 1000 \\\\
150 + q + q   &= 1000 \\\\
150 + 2 q     &= 1000 \\\\
2 q           &= 1000 - 150 \\\\
2 q           &= 850 \\\\
q             &= \dfrac {850} {2} \\\\
q             &= \dfrac {425 \times 2} {2} \\\\
q             &= \dfrac {425 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
q             &= 425
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
p  &= 150 + q \\\\
p  &= 150 + 425 \\\\
p  &= 575
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class="answercodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Answer Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">$p = 575, q= 425$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

$a$ and $b$ are two *different, positive* whole numbers which make the following statement true

$4a + 3b = 48$


Find two possible pairs of numbers which make the statement above true.

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">$a$ and $b$ are two *different, positive* whole numbers which make the following statement true
<emptyline>
$4a + 3b = 48$
<emptyline>

Find two possible pairs of numbers which make the statement above true.
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

Let's try for $a =12$

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times 12 + 3b                                &= 48 \\\\
48 + 3b                                         &= 48 \\\\
3b                                              &= 48 - 48 \\\\
b                                               &= 0
\end{aligned}
$

Let's try for $a =3$

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times 3 + 3b                                 &= 48 \\\\
12 + 3b                                         &= 48 \\\\
3b                                              &= 48 - 12 \\\\
3b                                              &= 36 \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {36} {3} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times 3} {3} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
b                                               &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$a=12, b=0 \\ a=3, b=12$

</div>
</div>

<div class="tipcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workingcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="workingcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Working Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">Let's try for $a =12$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times 12 + 3b                                &= 48 \\\\
48 + 3b                                         &= 48 \\\\
3b                                              &= 48 - 48 \\\\
b                                               &= 0
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's try for $a =3$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times 3 + 3b                                 &= 48 \\\\
12 + 3b                                         &= 48 \\\\
3b                                              &= 48 - 12 \\\\
3b                                              &= 36 \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {36} {3} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times 3} {3} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
b                                               &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answercodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

<div class="answercodeblock_title">

</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<div class="answercodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Answer Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">$a=12, b=0 \\ a=3, b=12$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

When you add up two numbers you get $11$, but when you multiply the two numbers you get $28$. What are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">When you add up two numbers you get $11$, but when you multiply the two numbers you get $28$. What are the two numbers?
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

Let the two numbers $= a$ and $b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned} 
a + b                         &= 11 \\\\
a \times b                    &= 28
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which meets the following critiera based on the equations above.

- The product of the $2$ numbers is $28$. 
- The sum of the $2$ numbers is $11$.

Factors of $28 = 1, 2, 4, 7, 14, 28$

Factor pairs of $28$ that add upto $11 = (7,4)$

| $a, b$    | $a \times b$  |    a + b          |
|:---------:|:-------------:|:-----------------:|
| $7, 4$    |   $28$        |       $11$        |

A quick check shows that $7$ and $4$ meets all the criteria above.

ABBR: 

*Note*: The two equations form a quadratic equation.

*Note*: This is a quadratic equation.

Solving quadratic equations is beyond the scope and any such equations can be easily inferred by trial and error method.


The two numbers are $4$ and $7$.
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$4$ and $7$

</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

I have forgotten the last two digits of my PIN number, but know that the first two digits are $8$ and $6$.

$8 \ 6 * *$

However,I know that my PIN number is divisible by $3, 4$ and $5$. What is my PIN number?

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">I have forgotten the last two digits of my PIN number, but know that the first two digits are $8$ and $6$.
<emptyline>
$8 \ 6 * *$
<emptyline>
However,I know that my PIN number is divisible by $3, 4$ and $5$. What is my PIN number?
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

LCM of $3, \ 4 \ \text{and} \ 5 = 60$

Since, Multiplying $60$ with any number will give $0$ at last digit. So, The last digit of the PIN is $0$.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {8700} {60} \\\\
&= \dfrac {6 \times 1450} {60} \\\\
&= \dfrac {6 \times 145 \times 10} {6 \times 10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{6} \times 145 \times \cancel{10}} {\cancel{6} \times \cancel{10}} \\\\
&= 145
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{As the remainder is } 0, \text{Subtracting } 60 \text{ from } 8700 &= 8700 - 60 \\\\
                                                                        &= 8640 
\end{aligned}
$

The PIN number is $= 8640$
</div>
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

Alternative solution

We can solve this my trial and error method using divisibility rule.

We know,

A number is divisible by $5$ if the last digit $0$ or $5$.

A number is divisible by $4$ if the last $2$ digits are divisible by $4$.

A number is divisible by $3$ if the cross addition is divisible by $3$.

Let's look at units place

$= 0$ (then only my PIN will be divisible by $4$)

Let's look at tens place

$\not 1$ (as $10$ is not divisible by $4$)

$= 2$ (as $20$ is divisible by $4$)

Let's now check if $8620$ is divisible by $3$

$= 8 + 6 + 2 + 0 = 16 \not$ divisible by $3$ 

The next multiple of $3$ would be $18$

$= 8 + 6 + 4 + 0 = 18$ 

$18$ is divisible by $3$

$40$ is divisible by $4$ and

$8640$ has $0$ at unit place, as such divisible by $5$.

My PIN number is $8640$.
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$8640$

</div>
</div>
<div class="workingscodeblock">
<div class="tipcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="tipcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Tips Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%

<emptyline>

                                                                        &= 8640

$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

In the sum below the letters $J, M$ and $C$ represent three different non-zero digits.

What is the value of $J + M + C \text{?}$

$
\begin{array}{cccc}
      & &J  &J \\
{}+   & &M  &M \\
{}+   & &C  &C \\
\hline
      &J  &M  &C \\ 
\end{array}
$

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">In the sum below the letters $J, M$ and $C$ represent three different non-zero digits.
<emptyline>
What is the value of $J + M + C \text{?}$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccc}
      & &J  &J \\
{}+   & &M  &M \\
{}+   & &C  &C \\
\hline
      &J  &M  &C \\ 
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

JJ, MM, CC are all multiples of $11$

If you add 3 2.digit numbers as in question then the highest number achieved is 

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
       &    & 9     & 9  \\
       &    & 8     & 8  \\
{}+    &    & 7     & 7 \\
\hline
       & 2  & 6     & 4
\end{array}
$

So, the largest value of $J$ can be $2$ or smallest value $1$

Now let's consider the Units places.

$J + M + C = C$, i.e. $J+M$ must add to $10$, then only $J+M+C$ will result in $C$ at untis place. 

Now refer to the Tens places, which has $1$ as carry over from sum of units places and $J + M = 10$ or $J + M$ will have $0$ in unit place.

$
\begin{aligned}
1 + J + M + C &= M \\
1 + 0 + C &= M \\
1 + C = M
\end{aligned}
$

To summarise the assumptions above: 

$
\begin{aligned}
J = 1 \text {\ or,} \\
J = 2 \\
J + M & = 10 \\
1 + C = M
\end{aligned}
$

Now let's substitute our assumptions

Assumption J = 1

$
\begin{aligned}
J + M &= 10 \\
1 + M &= 10 \\
    M &= 10 - 1  \\
      &= 9 \\
C + 1 &= M\\
C &= 9 -1 \\
  &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

If J is $1$, M is $9$ and C is $8$

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
       &    & 1     & 1  \\
       &    & 9     & 9  \\
{}+    &    & 8     & 8 \\
\hline
       & 1  & 9     & 8
\end{array}
$

Assumption J = 2

$
\begin{aligned}
J + M &= 10 \\
2 + M &= 10 \\
    M &= 10 - 2  \\
      &= 8 \\
C + 1 &= M\\
    C &= 8 -1 \\
      &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

If J is $2$, M is $8$ and C is $7$

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
       &    & 2     & 2  \\
       &    & 8     & 8  \\
{}+    &    & 7     & 7 \\
\hline
       & 2  & 8     & 7
\end{array}
$
</div>
</div>
